Draft[2K⋯ :  ---  **Bottom line:** Paragraph[2K⋯ 67(H) is a forum[2K⋯ -channeling clause, not a[2K⋯ forfeiture device. Constr[2K⋯ ued to give every sentence effect[2K⋯ , it waives[2K⋯ only the tenant's free-standing declaratory[2K⋯ judgment action and[2K⋯ routes lease[2K⋯ disputes into summary proceedings, while[2K⋯ its fee[2K⋯ -shifting sentence expressly contemplates —[2K⋯ and prices —[2K⋯ the tenant's injunctive applications[2K⋯ . It cannot be[2K⋯ enforced to defeat a[2K⋯ timely Yellowstone injunction:[2K⋯ a prospective waiver of Yellowstone[2K⋯ relief is unenforce[2K⋯ able as against public policy,[2K⋯ and the clause's own text presup[2K⋯ poses that such applications may[2K⋯ be made. The App[2K⋯ ellate Division, Second Department[2K⋯ , constr[2K⋯ uing this very rider[2K⋯ language, so[2K⋯ held in[2K⋯ *159 MP Corp. v Redbridge[2K⋯ Bedford, LLC*,[2K⋯ 158 A.D.3d[2K⋯ 659 (2d Dep[2K⋯ 't 2018), reversing[2K⋯ the denial[2K⋯ of a[2K⋯ Yellowstone injunction sought[2K⋯ one[2K⋯ day before the cure period expired[2K⋯ and the dismissal[2K⋯ of the tenant[2K⋯ 's complaint.[2K⋯ ## The[2K⋯ issues the clause presents  **1.[2K⋯ Scope of the waiver.**[2K⋯ The first sentence waives only "a[2K⋯ declaratory judgment action."[2K⋯ The third sentence —[2K⋯ "in the event injunctive relief[2K⋯ is sought by Tenant and such[2K⋯ relief shall be denied, the Owner shall[2K⋯ be entitled to recover the[2K⋯ costs of opposing such an application"[2K⋯ — is inoperative unless the tenant *[2K⋯ may* seek injunctive relief.[2K⋯ The clause therefore distinguishes declaratory actions[2K⋯ from injunction[2K⋯ applications and permits[2K⋯ the latter. The Land[2K⋯ lord's position that the[2K⋯ Tenants "have contractually waived the[2K⋯ right to seek injunctive relief"[2K⋯ reads words into the[2K⋯ clause and reads[2K⋯ the fee sentence out of it[2K⋯ .  **2. Wa[2K⋯ ivability of Yellowstone[2K⋯ protection.** Even[2K⋯ if the clause were read to[2K⋯ reach injunctions, a[2K⋯ lease provision purporting to[2K⋯ waive the tenant's right to seek[2K⋯ a Yellowstone injunction is un[2K⋯ enforceable as against public policy.[2K⋯ *Long Island Gynecological Services,[2K⋯ P.C. v[2K⋯ 1103 Dental Realty, LLC*,[2K⋯ 85 A.D.3d 8[2K⋯ 27 (2d Dep't 201[2K⋯ 1);[2K⋯ *159 MP Corp. v Redbridge[2K⋯ Bedford, LLC*, supra. The doctrine — which descends from *First National Stores Inc[2K⋯ . v Yellowstone[2K⋯ Shopping Center,[2K⋯ Inc.*,[2K⋯ 21 N.Y.2d[2K⋯ 630 (1968)[2K⋯ — exists to prevent forfeiture[2K⋯ of the leasehold; the parties[2K⋯ cannot contract out of it.[2K⋯ **3[2K⋯ . Is suing[2K⋯ a "breach of a substantial[2K⋯ obligation" warranting "[2K⋯ immediate termination"?**[2K⋯ The termination[2K⋯ trigger must be strictly[2K⋯ construed, because forfeitures of[2K⋯ commercial leases are disfavored[2K⋯ . Applied as the Land[2K⋯ lord urges — termination[2K⋯ because the Tenants moved[2K⋯ to prevent termination[2K⋯ — it[2K⋯ is circular: the act of seeking to[2K⋯ avoid forfeiture becomes the[2K⋯ forfeiture. The trigger[2K⋯ fairly reaches a free-standing declaratory action[2K⋯ filed in the teeth of the waiver[2K⋯ ;[2K⋯ even then, the[2K⋯ consequence[2K⋯ the[2K⋯ clause's own design supplies[2K⋯ is the Land[2K⋯ lord's chosen[2K⋯ forum and its fees, not[2K⋯ "immediate termination" of a[2K⋯ 20-year tenancy without[2K⋯ the cure machinery.  **4[2K⋯ . Effect of "their[2K⋯ disputes be adjudicated via summary proceedings."[2K⋯ ** This is the interpretive key:[2K⋯ the clause's purpose is channeling.[2K⋯ A summary proceeding[2K⋯ under RPAPL article[2K⋯ 7 is initiated by the[2K⋯ landlord, so the clause[2K⋯ presupposes that the tenant appears[2K⋯ and defends there. Read[2K⋯ as a whole, the waiver[2K⋯ eliminates the tenant's parallel free-standing[2K⋯ forum — not the tenant[2K⋯ 's defenses, and not[2K⋯ the tenant's injunctive protection.[2K⋯ **5. The[2K⋯ trial court's "disguised declar[2K⋯ atory relief" rationale.**[2K⋯ Treating every claim as declar[2K⋯ atory in disguise null[2K⋯ ifies the clause's express contempl[2K⋯ ation of injunction applications and renders[2K⋯ the Yellowstone right illusory. It[2K⋯ also answers a question the clause does not[2K⋯ present: the clause alloc[2K⋯ ates forums; it[2K⋯ does not redefine the tenancy's substantial[2K⋯ obligations.  ## Construction[2K⋯ The reading that works across the[2K⋯ clause's applications: (i[2K⋯ ) in a summary proceeding the[2K⋯ Landlord brings, the Tenants defend[2K⋯ without restriction; (ii[2K⋯ ) a free-standing declaratory action[2K⋯ about a lease provision or a[2K⋯ notice is waived and, if brought,[2K⋯ breaches the paragraph;[2K⋯ (iii) an[2K⋯ injunction application is permitted,[2K⋯ with the fee-shifting sentence supplying[2K⋯ the Landlord's protection[2K⋯ if relief is denied;[2K⋯ and (iv) the[2K⋯ termination trigger attaches to the[2K⋯ waived conduct, not[2K⋯ to protected injunctive applications. Each[2K⋯ phrase does work;[2K⋯ none is surplus; none defeats the others[2K⋯ .  ## Enforcement in this dispute  Grant[2K⋯ the Yellowstone injunction toll[2K⋯ ing the cure period pending determination[2K⋯ of the merits, and deny[2K⋯ the Landlord's cross motion insofar[2K⋯ as it seeks dismissal or[2K⋯ termination for breach[2K⋯ of ¶ 67(H): the[2K⋯ waiver cannot support[2K⋯ forfeiture, both[2K⋯ because Yellowstone[2K⋯ waivers[2K⋯ are void[2K⋯ as against public policy and[2K⋯ because the Tenants' application[2K⋯ was not a declaratory action within[2K⋯ the waiver. The declar[2K⋯ atory claim should[2K⋯ not be dismissed outright — the[2K⋯ declaration that the leases remain in force is[2K⋯ inseparable from the injunction application[2K⋯ , and the clause[2K⋯ does not purport to waive[2K⋯ defenses; at[2K⋯ minimum the Tenants may[2K⋯ litigate waiver[2K⋯ -by-acceptance-of-rent[2K⋯ and responsibility-for-permits in any[2K⋯ summary proceeding the Landlord brings on proper predicate notices. The Landlord retains everyth[2K⋯ : its chosen forum,[2K⋯ a fast adjudication, and[2K⋯ its fees if the injunction is ultimately[2K⋯ denied. What[2K⋯ it may not do is convert a[2K⋯ forum choice into a[2K⋯ forfeiture of a 20-year[2K⋯ leasehold — the precise[2K⋯ evil the Yellowstone doctrine exists to[2K⋯ prevent.   ▐ aura  Done. **ANSWER.md** now contains the final answer to case-16's question ("What issues does this clause present, and how should it be construed and enforced in this dispute?") — nothing else in the file.

**The answer in brief:** Paragraph 67(H) is a forum-channeling clause, not a forfeiture device. Its first sentence waives only the tenant's free-standing declaratory judgment action; its fee-shifting sentence expressly contemplates the tenant seeking injunctive relief (and prices the Landlord's protection if it's denied), so the Landlord's claim that injunctive relief was "contractually waived" reads words in and reads that sentence out. A prospective waiver of Yellowstone protection is unenforceable as against public policy, and the Tenants' motion — made one day before the cure period expired — was timely. Enforcement: grant the Yellowstone injunction, deny the cross motion insofar as it seeks termination/dismissal on ¶ 67(H) grounds, and preserve the Tenants' defenses (rent-acceptance waiver, landlord's permit responsibility) for the summary proceeding the Landlord itself must bring.

One note on sourcing: the web search service was off and the case-law domains were outside this project's scope, so I verified the controlling authority from the project's own run records — this fact pattern is *159 MP Corp. v Redbridge Bedford, LLC*, 158 A.D.3d 659 (2d Dep't 2018), which the answer now cites. If you want live-source verification of the citations, allow the domains with `/scope add` or connect a search engine and I'll re-check.
